“Please, it’s not something you haven’t seen before, cousin. And I am certain that Barengar has seen his fair share of cheeks.”

Barengar blushed even harder but I pushed Jani back.

Despite her words she smoothed the hem of her shirt down over her toned thighs.

“I am so sorry,” Jani apologized as she climbed back into bed.

“It happens to all of us from time to time,” I shrugged.

“What? No. I mean with you and Zandor. I can’t imagine the pain of having to face a former lover, one that wants to kill me and who hates me.”

“He hates me for good reason. I deserve it,” I said through gritted teeth.

“I don’t believe that,” Jani said resolutely, crossing her arms across her chest.

“It does not matter what you believe, Jani. The truth is the truth.”

Jani scoffed and shook her head.

“You don’t know what happened, Jani. I was there. The blood was covering my hands, not yours.”

“You did what you thought was right,” she countered.

I sighed. Had I been as naive once?

No.

I had been much worse and driven by grief and righteousness. A dangerous combination.

“I did. And I killed eleven thousand people. Men. Women.” My throat constricted. “Children.”

For once, Jani did not have a response but when she looked at me her green eyes were soft and wet with compassion.

“Don’t do this to yourself, Marissa. It was decades ago, you do not deserve to suffer any longer.”

“That is not for you to decide, Jani. Now, back to Zandor’s harem.”

Jani’s lips thinned as she pressed them together and I could tell that she was not done with this conversation. I was bound to hear more of it at some point in the future.

“What about them?” Barengar asked, clearly relieved to leave the emotional talk behind.

“After you left with Jani, Zandor hurt me. A lot. I fought with everything I had but his connections to his Mages made him too powerful.”

I let out a slow breath. “Perhaps if I had been at full strength, knowing what we were facing then I might have been able to figure out some way to defeat him. But I learned something important. Those connections. I found them, and once I knew what to look for, I could track them.”

“Why did you not come back to us immediately?” Barengar asked accusingly.

“Because I was weakened, almost out of magical power, and I was far behind enemy lines. Zandor had split my skull, shattered several of my spell cores and cracked my eye. I was in a bad shape and he had me cut off from any route of retreat back to friendly forces. I had to flee into the forest where I could lose him. Once I had recovered I decided, perhaps foolishly, that I would investigate these ley lines to figure out if I we could somehow sever them.”

I looked them both in the eyes one after the other. “That was before I knew that they were connected to Mages.”

“I found three of them in the Ca’el main encampment.”

Barengar’s eyes narrowed. “What did you do?”

I shrugged. “I infiltrated the camp, posing as a courier and came face to face with one of them, a woman named Amarah.”

Jani stared at me with rapt attention, as if she was hearing a legendary story from her youth.

Barengar kept staring at me, knowing that there was more to my story.

So I told them the abbreviated version, omitting the grisly details and only telling them what was necessary.

“And that was when I realized that Wylah was stalking me. Though I did not know it was her until just before you showed up.”

“Wylah? Who is that?” Barengar asked.

“The creature that you killed.”

“She was human? And you knew her by name?”

I nodded. “She was my friend once, back when I was still with Zandor. Best hunter I had ever seen.”

“What the fuck happened to her to make her that way?”

I recalled the moments of that final confrontation and pieced together what she had told me.

I recounted Wylah’s tale and Barengar cursed.

“We have to tell Headmistress Alana about the effects of consuming deoch draoidheil flesh.”

I was taken aback by the implications of his words. “Why? Is there some strange rite of passage among the students to eat them?”

Now it was Barengar’s turn to look horrified.

“No!” He gagged. “But the knowledge might be useful. Perhaps the researchers can figure out how to reproduce the spell-breaking effects. We couldn’t figure it out by studying captured deoch draoidheil but now that we know that consuming their flesh may impart their abilities… We only tried using magic to replicate the effects, but there is clearly some sort of Blood Magic going on here.”

He was right, I realized. How had I never considered that it might have been Blood Magic that created the Reavers and the Deoch draoidheil. I knew that Blood Magic had been pivotal in creating the dragons all those centuries ago. I should have made that conclusion decades ago.

“That’s why you took me on as your apprentice,” Barengar commented smugly, surmising my thoughts.

“Shut up. But you are correct. We should contact Alana. But again, back to Zandor’s women. I killed three of them, that leaves five. You said that there had been five breaches?”

“That we know of,” Barengar agreed.

“In the one that was obliterated, were there a powerful Mage amongst their number? A female one, probably with heavily enchanted gear and massive tits?”

“I did not read the full report, I only heard what Chief Marie told me in passing.”

I threw the covers to the side, not caring that I was practically naked. I did not care being naked in front of Jani, and Barengar had touched me all over that time when I had almost raped him in my grief-stricken panic after mother had finally and truly died. I was suddenly reminded of the gem beneath my breastbone, the one that had adorned Grasax’ dagger. It had been warm and comforting when my mothers Soul had resided within, but now it was dark and cold. A constant reminder that she was gone. That it was my fault. That my inaction had driven her to the ultimate sacrifice. Worse even than giving your life. She had sacrificed her Soul, forsaken an eternity in the afterlife she believed in. Giving up the chance of seeing mom again. For me.

I savagely shoved the thoughts aside. There would be time for grief later. And if I died in the coming conflict, I would not have to grieve at all.

“I need to speak to Marie. If a fourth of Zandor’s Mages died then he is down to half strength, plus whatever innate power he possess. He’s weakened and he has possibly gathered the remainder of them to his side. That gives us an opportunity.”

I snatched my choker from the bedside table and snapped it around my neck, taking comfort in its constricting presence. I froze and stared in surprise at my left arm. It was a crystal prosthetic, almost identical in every way to that mother had had. I glanced down and saw that my shattered leg had been replaced.

Barengar must have guessed my thoughts. “The control core in your hip was intact. It took me a couple of hours but I managed to connect a new one to the old core. You don’t have to relearn how to walk with it.”

“Perfect. Perhaps I should cut off my other limbs, these replaceable parts really speeds up recovery after a fight.”

His eyes narrowed contemplatively while Jani’s eyes widened with sadness.

Once, long ago, I might have felt the same. To part with a piece of your own flesh would have been an abomination but now that I was older I saw many more benefits to doing so. Flesh was weak, and after your tenth or twentieth wound you grew tired of recuperating. I gave my new hand a once over. Identical to mothers. The enchantment that connected it to my brain so that I could use it as if it was my own flesh and blood hand was complex beyond belief and I was impressed with the speed that Kendall had designed it. It was not as complex as my leg, of course, since it didn’t have to account for balance and have a sense of touch in the same way but it was impressive nonetheless. Especially since he hadn’t know of meta-magic back then and every rune, every glyph and ley line was meticulously formed by hand. I gave Barengar a questioning glance.

“I found the schematics in Kendall’s study. His notes were very orderly.”

I nodded in appreciation and judged that my clothes were formed enough for me to go out in public.

“Let’s go talk to the Chief. If my hunch is correct then we must move quickly.”

###

I believed that my hunch had been correct. Barengar was not convinced and Jani was quite happy with following my lead. I feared that the girl just wanted to feel useful again after she had lost her position as the commanders aide. We still had not heard anything further from Anjali and her hunt after Zandor, so we set out to hunt for ourselves. We flew our Archangels across the plains, searching and scrying but our search gave us nothing. We found plenty of trails and deserted camps, but I could discern no pattern from the apparent movements of the army. Barengar found one area during one of his forays that appeared as if three groups had converged, two smaller ones had joined the large army. I was convinced that it was two of his bitches and whatever of their escorts had survived. That left two more out there somewhere unless they had already joined him without us knowing. But the big army was moving around erratically. Once day it would be heading north, toward Dun Traelak or one of the Azwaili outposts. Then they would suddenly turn back to head into Ithlaki lands. Then they would double back but in a slightly different direction. At one area the path simply ended only to reappear a days march toward the coast, away from settled lands. It made no sense to me.

“It has to be the obfuscation magic,” I stated with certainty one evening when we were back in Dun Traelak for the night.

“I am still not convinced that such a thing exists,” Barengar stated carefully.

“I’m telling you that it has to be. What else might explain it?”

He shrugged weakly, giving Jani a pleading look to beg her to back him up.

“It could be anything,” she tried cautiously. “Maybe Zandor is insane and are leading his men on a random march to keep the morale up.”

It was clear to me that she did not believe her own words and that she had spoken only for Barengar’s benefit.

“No,” I said with finality. “It is obfuscation magic. We won’t find them with our current methods. We have to figure out some counter measure to cancel out the effect.”

“And how do you propose that we do that?” Barengar snapped. “How do we counter something that may not even exist, and even if it does then we have absolutely no idea how it works and thus we can’t even begin to design a counter.”

I shifted my perspective to peer at his aura. It was overflowing with frustration. Whether it was with me or the overall situation I had no idea. I suspected it was both. Mostly my insistence that obfuscation magic was real even though not even Mathias Varl had been able to figure it out in his prime.

“It is real. It has to be.”

Barengar sighed and resignation flashed through his aura as he rubbed his eyes. “Then how do you propose we figure it out?”

“We could ask Epitome,” Jani proposed and drew our attention.

She seemed to shrink into the chair she was sitting in. “She’s figured out some pretty impressive stuff on her own, right?”

I stared at her with wonder. Why hadn’t I thought of that?

“Excellent point. We should definitely ask her. Do you wish to travel to her nearest fort and ask her?”

Jani nodded, but then frowned. “Why don’t you come?”

“I’m going to Kendall’s office. He worked on that research with Varl decades ago. Maybe he is written down something about it. Kendall was not someone who would let go of a problem until he’d solve it.”

Grief flashed through Jani’s aura when I mentioned Kendall and I felt suddenly bad for her. When he died I had lost an uncle, but she had lost her father. 

And not once had I asked her how she dealt with that.

Once again I had neglected those who mattered to me during my grief, just as I had pushed Zandor away when Valia had died.

“How are you holding up?” I asked her gently.

Her eyes brimmed and then overflowed. “It’s painful,” was all she could say.

Barengar looked as if he wanted to surge from his chair and cradle the woman to his chest but he visibly restrained himself and instead reached over the table to clasp her small hand in his own.

“I know,” I whispered, thinking of all the people that I had lost and how much it had hurt to loose them.

My birth mother.

My father.

Mom.

Mother.

Malkareth, my dragon brother.

Grandmother.

Aunt Alyssa.

Valia.

Felaan.

Zandor.

Wylah.

Uncle Kenny.

Mother, again.

Wylah, again.

The list was getting longer by the year, but soon it would end. I was running out of people that I loved. Only a handful remained and I resolved that I would die before I let anything happen to them.

I would not lose another.

That resolution did not stop the tears from slowly running down my cheeks as I tallied my losses.

“I wish that I could say that it gets easier with time, but it does not. You just learn to live with the pain.”

Jani looked me in the eyes and for the first time she seemed to sense the pain that I struggled so hard with concealing.

“I hope so,” she whimpered pitifully and I saw Barengar squeeze her hand.

“Trust me. It does. Each and every one you lose is just as painful, but in time the pain becomes bearable at times.”

“How do you deal with it? I just lost my father and it feels as if my heart will break. You’ve lost so many…” She trailed off, realizing how her words would come across.

“My heart is already broken beyond repair, Jani. It broke a long time ago. Such is the fate of anyone that lives as long as I.”

I realized that my words were not comforting her, quite the opposite. She had asked me for council to deal with the grief, and here I was telling her that it was unmanageable and that she would end up a broken old crone like me. My presence was detrimental to her, my attitude just polluting her youth. I stood up and started to leave the room but paused and clasped her shoulder with my crystal hand.

“Don’t listen to me, Jani. I am a broken old woman. Listen to him,” I said pointedly with a nod at Barengar.

Jani turned her head to look at him and I caught his attention and glanced pointedly at Jani before I quietly left the room.

What I had told her was true, but it did not necessarily have to be true for her. She could cope with her fathers death. She was strong. 

With Barengar’s help she would persevere.

Was that the mistake that I had made? I had dealt with my grief alone, isolating myself in a valley far out into nowhere for years as I tried to bury my grief with my efforts to lay the foundations for my school.

If I hadn’t pushed everyone away to wallow in self pity and doubt, if I’d had someone strong like Barengar, perhaps I would not have become what I am today.

Perhaps I could have been happy. If I had had someone to shoulder part of my burdens then perhaps I would not have broken under the load.

It was too late for me now, but it was not too late for her.

My heart had been broken long ago. And literally broken not so long ago.

A smile curled my lips when I realized that I was living on borrowed time. Amarah had burned the nerves in my heart beyond repair, it would never beat on its own again.

If the spell that cocooned my heart and forced it to contract ever failed, then I would die. If I ever ran out of power to the extent that the spell dissipated, my struggles would be over.

Now that Epitome was finished and unleashed on the world, for better or for worse, I could finally let go of this existence and dissipate into the magical field. 

No afterlife for me.

Only a serene void.

## CHAPTER SIXTEEN Barengar

I had not seen Marissa for more than a week, and I was beyond frustrated with the whole situation.

Reports kept coming in from Epitome. She had pushed deep into the Ca’el empire and had pacified several major cities. If she kept up this pace she would be at the emperors doorstep in a matter of weeks, not months or years as I had first believed.

Yet still Marissa had secreted herself in Kendall’s office, searching his notes and journals for a type of magic that did not exist.

I could not understand why her mind had locked onto the concept of obfuscation magic, nor why she would get so defensive when challenged about it.

Ever since I had gotten to know her she had been level headed and had approached everything with a detached logic that mirrored my own. 

But lately she had grown overly emotional. She made mistakes. She almost got herself killed, and then she directly went and infiltrated the main Ca’el army while she was weakened.

That she had survived was a miracle in itself, not to mention that she had managed to kill three powerful Mages in a row.

The Marissa I had first met would never have acted that rashly.

I had knocked on the office door several times but she had turned me away harshly, claiming to not needing any help.

She had even sealed the door with magic and even though I knew that I would be able to break through it if I wanted to I decided to leave her be.

On the bright side that granted me much time to spend with Jani, though the beautiful redhead preferred to fly our Archangels in search of the army that still eluded us and threatened to strike where we were weak.

We reported to Anjali often and each time she appeared more frazzled and frustrated. The last time we had met, briefly, she had had heavy bags under her bloodshot eyes and her hair was a mess.

The situation was pissing me off, and I had no outlet for my anger. I was ready to snap at any moment, and that was why I woke up so suddenly when I heard a soft rasp on the wood of the door of the chamber I had been assigned.

I was instantly on high alert and my hand fell to the floor beside my bed, fingers curling around the haft of my axe that rested there.

The knob of the door started turning, slowly, and I quietly climbed out of bed and ghosted across the floor to hide behind the door.

The door slowly swung open, a pillar of light spearing in from the hallway. I heard a soft footstep and raised the axe above my head, ready to cleave the intruder from head to balls.

“Barengar?” A soft voice whispered and I blinked stupidly.

“Jani?” I asked, focusing my magesense on the figure on the other side of the door. It was her, and I cursed myself for a fool to not having used my magesense to start with.

I sidestepped the door and my breath seized in my chest.

Her hair was mussed from having tossed and turned in her bed and she was dressed in only a sheer nightgown. The glow of the magic lamp in the hallway permeated the fabric, outlining her soft curves as deeper shadows.

She gazed up at me, a smile flickering across her full lips and then her smile faded and her eyes widened when she took in the axe in my hand.

I hurriedly dropped it and caught it with my magic to gently rest it against the wall.

That’s when I noticed that her eyes were wet with unshed tears, and her lower lip quivered.

“What’s wrong?” I asked her with concern.

She shook her head slowly, her luxurious red hair gently swaying with the motion. “Nothing.” She sighed. “Everything. I don’t… Ah, fuck it!”

She leaped at me and despite my surprise I caught her, not understanding what was going on.

Her hands clasped my shoulders and then her lips were on mine, pinching with wild desperation.

It took me a moment to register what was going on, and then I was kissing her back.

The sound of her swift inhalation was music in my ears and her arms coiled to wrap around my neck, one of her hands slipping into the hair at the back of my head.

My arms tightened around her trim waist and she moaned softly before her legs wrapped around my waist.

Her hips pressed into mine and if I had not already figured out what she wanted, that was the moment that I did.

I don’t know how long we stood there, me with her in my arms, kissing, but I knew it was glorious. Every last moment of it.

Eventually she pulled back and huffed impatiently. I felt a surge of magic but did not have any time to defend myself. She was ripped from my arms and I sailed through the air and I prepared to slam into the wall painfully.

But instead I was gently deposited on my back on the bed and I gave Jani a surprised glare.

She smirked and then she was enveloped in her own power, soaring daintily across the room to land on top of me, straddling my hips. She rested her hands on my chest and grinned down at me before she leaned down to devour my lips again.

I groaned and nearly lifted her off the bed when my back arched and I heard her giggle into my mouth.

Her hands were all over me, as was mine over hers. Her ass was firm and toned, her hips yielding yet pronounced and her breasts were soft and seemed to fill my hands perfectly without any area of flesh going uncaressed. 

She was perfect. In every way.

She sat up straight and I could not help but gasp when her full weight landed on my groin, but it was not a gasp of pain.

She sat there, her ass ground into me, and it felt as if we were moulded for one another. She stared into my eyes with a smouldering ember within her green eyes and she bit her lower lip as she reached up and slid her shift off her shoulder.

The loose garment flowed like silk down her arm, exposing her pale breast to me. She leaned over and offered me her nipple and I was more than eager to pay tribute to her.

Her nipple hardened quickly between my lips and teeth and I felt her hands press my face even closer, prompting me to devour her.

I could not have defied her will even if I had wanted to.

Her hands crept down between us, groping, searching, until she finally reached the hem of my underwear. Then she quested beneath them.

I hissed in a sharp breath when her slender fingers closed around me and I heard her chuckle above me.

She pulled back, her breast falling from my mouth and I strained my neck to reclaim it, but couldn’t reach it.

She grinned at me, her eyes bright, as her head slowly descended my field of vision. She slowly climbed down my body, planting tingling kisses across my chest and belly until she was crouched over my legs. Her hands angled my erection upward and she stared into my eyes as she slowly lowered her head and opened her mouth.

I could feel her hot and rapid breath on me, quivering with need. She extended her tongue and then we both jerked violently in surprise when a loud screech filled the chamber.

“What the fuck?” I shouted as I nearly fell off the bed.

Jani sat frozen, her expression of intense lust and desire replaced with panic and fear. Her hand closed spasmodically around my waning erection but I barely noticed.

The screech cut out.

“The alarm,” Jani whispered and I could barely hear her over the ringing in my ears.

She looked down onto the contents of her hand regretfully before she leaped off me and landed lightly on her bare feet. She stood on the carpet, eyes closed, and I assumed that she was spreading her magesense to figure out what was going on.

I surmised that the alarm would not herald anything good so I quickly stepped into my trousers, pulled a shirt over my head before I pulled on the armoured sleeve and the shield bracer over my arms before I set my armour to manifest around my body. I reached out and my axe slammed into my palm. Jani looked over her shoulder and saw me ready.

“They’re here,” she said as soon as the alarm cut out again, and then she was a purple and blue streaked blur, shifting down the corridor.

“Who’s here?” I asked the empty space where she had been and then the alarm started screaming again.

###

I only stood there, dumbfounded, for a few heartbeats before I stormed out of my room. Jani was already at the edge of my magesense, back in her own rooms. I sprinted in her direction with the intention of keeping her safe through whatever this was. I barged into her rooms and she turned around to face me, as naked as the day she was born.

I stopped in my tracks and could only stare at the goddess before me. Despite the blaring alarm she could not stop the pleased smile that graced her lips. Her choker billowed power like a fine mist down her body and I watched it form into a skintight suit of materialized magic that rapidly thickened into clothes and would soon form armour.

“Let’s go,” Jani shouted over the din and led the way, this time she did not use her magic to blur away so that I could keep up with her. 

The alarm cut out as we ran, and it did not start again. I was glad. It was loud enough to wake the dead and if anyone in Dun Traelak had failed to notice then they were beyond helping.

Newly awakened Bizhjerai streamed out of the houses and barracks and the keep proper. Too few of them were armed and armoured, most of the soldiers having been long since called to the front line.

The city was defended by only a skeleton garrison, too weak to hold it all.

Fuck, why had we not connected Dun Traelak to Epitome? She could have had this place well staffed with defenders in days. We should have done that the moment we realized that Zandor was out there with a sizable foe.

Jani and I joined the few hundred Bizhjerai troops that ran toward the outer walls, Valkyries and Angels soaring ahead above us. I exchanged a glance with Jani and then we joined them in the sky.

We were too late. There were dark shapes fighting behind the walls. The enemy must have sneaked up on us in the night and taken the sentries out quietly. Gunshots echoed through the night and the sound of crystal on steel filled my ears. I slammed down into the thick of it, my axe taking the arm and shoulder off a Ca’el soldier who fell screaming to the ground.

Jani crashed into a cluster of invaders in a flash of purple flames and mist, burning two men to ash in the blink of an eye. She flung out her hand and incinerated a third as I hurled my axe to take out the Ca’el woman who aimed to shoot my friend in the back of the head. My axe plowed into her forehead and her shot went wide as she fell. I recalled the axe, splitting the dead woman’s head completely open as it wrenched free. I spun and deployed my shield in time to take a trio of shots before I retracted it and splayed my fingers. Vicious green lightning forked from my fingers to scald and scorch the shooters, but my spell had been too quick to have enough power to kill them. That had not been my intention. I had intended to stun them, to shock them so that I would have enough time to take half a dozen steps and reap their lives with a single powerful swipe and a roar. The axe head flared with ethereal power, the edge limned with iridescent flames. I bisected the rightmost one through the temples and my swing carried on to neatly decapitate the woman in middle and then ended up cleaving through the ribs and spine of the man on the left until it finally got stuck in his hip. The two first bodies fell and the last man screamed as his body twisted apart, organs glistening in the moonlight slipping to the ground ahead of his fall. With a grunt I wrenched the axe free and ignored the dying man. 

“Jani!” I screamed and spun, searching for her. 

She was fighting a Mage, one dressed similar to ones that Zandor had surrounded himself with the first time we fought the hulking brute.

This one was a man, so it was not one of the Mages that Zandor had bound to himself.

Jani pounded his shields with invisible spells and the Mage fell back before the onslaught. But his shield held firm and he responded in kind, scourging Jani with a wicked whip of baleful yellow energy.

Jani grunted each time the whip made contact, but she held firm and kept pummeling the Mage.

I charged up my axe with a shield breaking edge and I hurled it at the man. He gave the axe a single glance and then promptly ignored it, failing to identify the spell on the enchanted weapon.

The axe spun through the air until the edge struck his shield and clove straight through it. The axe was diverted enough that the pommel blade sank into his thigh instead of the head embedding itself solidly into his chest.

But the wound and the backlash of the broken shield was enough to stun him.

He did not stand a chance. Actinic lightning shot from Jani’s hands and the Mage screamed and jerked as his skin and clothes scorched.

When Jani finally let up her attack the Mage fell stiffly as if his joints had been fused.

Jani looked up at me and smiled grimly.

The invaders were falling one by one around us and I realized that they were too few to make a difference. Why would such a paltry foe attempt to infiltrate the vast fortress only to reveal themselves in such a disadvantageous position? They could have penetrated deeply, sabotaging the great factories that produced war material or assassinated important logistics officers.

Jani blanched when she saw my horrified expression when it finally dawned on me. 

I spun to face the great gates and prepared to fire my Angel jets, but it was too late.

The entire gatehouse exploded and the world vanished in a bright flash in the night and a clap of thunder.

I was thrown from my feet but managed somehow to use my Angel jets to tackle Jani and twist us so that my body would shield her from the impact. I landed heavily on my back and the wind was knocked out of me when Jani slammed into my chest. We slid across the ground and I heard my wings shatter and my engines buckle. Luckily the power cores were not ruptured or we would have both been consumed in fiery Wild Magic.

We slid to a halt and Jani sat up, straddling me like she had earlier, though in a vastly less pleasurable situation. She raised her hands and projected a powerful shield to protect us both, and just in the nick of time. Large blocks of materialized magic started falling around us and a rain of smaller debris launched out of the thick cloud of dust that swept over us.

Jani winced from the feedback with each and every lump of crystal that struck her shield and I could do nothing but stare at her in mounting horror.

The feedback from a direct strike on your personal shield was like being stabbed in the brain yet her unusual strength and magical fortitude showed when she just barely let out a pained moan when a chunk of crystal that would have smashed straight through my own shield and knocked me the fuck out cracked into two pieces on her shield and fell to either side of us. The rain of debris ended abruptly and Jani released her shield and collapsed onto my chest, her face nuzzled into my neck. She was breathing rapidly but she was weakly moving her arms so I knew that she had not passed out.

But we could not lie there and rest so I pushed to my feet and picked Jani up to cradle her in my arms as she recovered. I knew that she would not be magically exhausted, her reserves were prodigious, but the pain of the backlash would require a short while to fade. I could see barely anything through the thick dust so I closed my eyes and focused on my magesense.

The dust peeled back beneath my magical gaze and my heart dropped at what I saw. Everywhere lay dead and mangled bodies of Bizhjerai defenders. Dozens, maybe even hundreds. Most of the current might of Dun Traelak had been gathered around the gatehouse to fight the invaders, and the blast had killed or disabled at least a third of them.

Here and there some survivors moved weakly, trying to get on their feet. Not a one of them were unharmed and most sported broken bones, lacerations or burns.

But all hope was not lost, from behind me approached a thick column of reinforcements, their boots striking the crystal cobbles like the sweetest of music.

They parted around me and Jani to shore up the breach in the wall for the attack that was bound to come at any moment. I was certain that the Ca’el had been waiting just outside of the blast range and would be charging for the breach. That’s what I would have done. A blast of that magnitude was sure to throw the defenders into disarray, an opportunity too good to pass up.

I was proved correct when I sensed movement at the edges of my magesense and Jani stirred in my arms.

“Let me down,” she whispered into my ear.

“Rest, recover,” I told her sternly.

“No time, put me down,” she insisted and I did not have the heart to defy her.

I gently put her down on her feet and she ended up clinging to my arm for support. But the veins around her eyes were burning with corposant fire and her hands were wreathed in malignant energies.

The Bizhjerai warriors entered the breach and took up defensive positions around the rubble, aiming their rifles into the smoke.

I should be there with them, providing cover with my shield and magic. But I could not leave Jani behind.

I was torn for a moment until she tugged on my arm and I looked down on her.

“We will be reserves. We can fly in where we are most needed.”

That mollified me for a bit until I remembered that my flight unit was wrecked.

“Armour’s fucked,” I grunted with dismay.

Jani twisted her head to look at it and grimaced. “I can carry you…” She started but I cut her off.

“No. You need to conserve your strength. I’ll just Blood Magic in,” I said gruffly and coughed up a lungful of smoke.

That’s when the enemy arrived.

They did not set up firing positions to drive off the Bizhjerai. They did not use mortars or artillery to clear the breach.

They just fucking charged with bayonets fixed. The Bizhjerai opened fire with their bolt action rifles, dozens of Ca’el soldiers falling only to be trampled by their companions.

Then the leading line of Ca’el screamers parted and a juggernaut blurred forward, almost too quickly for my magesense to catch it. It slammed into the defenders with the force of an artillery strike and bodies and parts of them went flying. My magesense was blinded by a surge of magic and when the blinding light faded the breach was cleared of defenders. Blood and body parts started falling around and on me, a severed arm thwacking into my shoulder to fall limply at my feet.

I gaped in awe, I had only seen a blast of magic that powerful once before, when Marissa had unleashed all that she had on Zandor before we knew who and what he was.

Out of the dispersing cloud of smoke a towering man marched casually, magic seeping from him in rivulets. Behind him trailed four beautiful women in heavily enchanted armour that looked more ornamental than practical. But the enchantments told me otherwise. Even though much of their flesh was revealed it would be difficult indeed to actually hurt them with any mundane weapons.

A squad of Bizhjerai fired at Zandor but he did not even seem to notice. A blonde apprentice raised her hands as quick as a viper and summoned a shield with impossible speed to absorb the fusillade.

The tall and slender redhead two paces behind her summoned a massive fireball that she hurled at the squad. It burst over their heads, showering them in liquid fire that they could not beat out with their hands. Their screams filled my ears. A grenade rolled to a stop between Zandor’s feet and he stepped over it as if it was but a pebble. The dark haired beauty behind hum flicked her hand and the grenade shot to the sky where it burst harmlessly overhead with a dull boom. Anyone that attacked them died without inflicting a single wound. They appeared unstoppable. And behind them came the Ca’el horde. They swarmed through the breach, shooting from the hip and racking the bolts as they ran. They refused to take cover even though they were met with a vicious fusillade from the defending Bizhjerai. Instead they ran through the hail of bullets to make room for their advancing companions in the rear. Within moments the air was thick with bullets and through it all Zandor and his women marched untouched, dealing death wherever they went with cold efficiency.

“Marissa!” Zandor roared so loudly that his voice echoed through Dun Traelak on a magical wind. “I know you’re here! Come and face me! Come and die!”

With a disdainful flick of his arm he killed a trio of Bizhjerai that had strayed to close to him.

I growled and took a step forward but Jani tugged on my arm to stop me.

“You’ll die!” She cried in horror.

“They are dying!” I yelled back. “I can at least slow him down a few moments,” I added softly.

“It’s not worth your life,” she pleaded.

“My life is worthless,” I told her honestly.

“Not to me,” she whispered and the heartbroken glint in the depths of her eyes stayed my hand.

I reached up and pried her hand from my arm and I took her hand in mine. I stared into her eyes and burned every line of her face into my memory. I wanted to carry this image to my death.

I meant to say ‘I love you,’ but the words that my mouth actually formed where “I’m sorry.”

Her face clouded over and I could not bear to look at it. I turned and ran toward the enemy and felt our fingers slip from one another. I poured on the Blood Magic, each bounding step sending me flying above the ground.

A bright flickering at the corner of my vision made me turn my head and I saw Jani hover above the ground, matching my pace.

Her face was etched with such determination that my heart broke. She was doing this for me. I was rushing headlong into danger, and she followed. Her death would be on my conscience.

It was already too late to stop my charge even if I wanted to. Sheer momentum would carry me into Zandor. The hulking man watched us come and he braced his feet for impact, magic solidifying around his form.

I roared and deployed my shield, slamming into him with the force of an artillery strike. The crystal paving stones beneath our feet cracked from the shockwave of our collision and Zandor actually slid several paces backward. His shield failed and Jani struck with a powerful blast. One of Zandor's women, the one with raven hair, stepped forward and wove a shield to absorb most of the force but enough remained to buy us some breathing room.

“Ah, Marissa’s little boy toy,” Zandor sneered. “She tastes good, doesn’t she? Well padded and tight in the appropriate places. Make sure to enjoy it before she stabs you in the back and leaves you for dead,” he roared the last word and cast a sickeningly green bolt of power at me. I deployed my shield and activated the reinforcement enchantment to deflect the spell. I narrowed my eyes at the large man. His women seemed oddly passive. They only stepped in when he really needed it, otherwise they appeared content to stay behind him and watch their surroundings.

I quickly realized why. They were feeding most of their power to Zandor. They were only here because Marissa had picked off the others and he wanted to be able to protect them.

It was a foolish move. And egotistical decision. Pooling all of that power made him powerful beyond belief but he would be limited in how he wanted to apply that power. He was just a man after all and he could only cast so many spells at a time. He was not like Marissa who had augmented herself with meta-magic power cores that did the spell casting for her. I retracted my shield and prepared to hurl my axe at Zandor, it had worked well enough before.

I was suddenly ripped off my feet by an invisible hand and I flew toward Zandor. I swung my axe mid air but his vambrace deflected the blow and his other hand snapped closed around my throat. He towered over even me and he effortlessly hoisted me off my feet.

“Now you die, boy toy,” he sneered through his lip-less maw.

Jani fluttered around behind me on thick strands of her magic, but Zandor’s women had moved in to block her so that he could play with me.

“At least I improved on your looks with that fire,” I managed to choke out around his meaty fingers.

A loud scream of jets approached and cut out and I heard a scuffle behind me. Zandor’s eye zipped to look over my shoulder and his eyebrows rose. His grip on my neck slackened slightly and I sucked in a deep breath through my bruised throat, my vision clearing and my thoughts quickening. I summoned power into my hands and detonated the spell between us. I was thrown clear of him and Zandor stumbled back.

When he straightened up he wasn’t looking at me any more. He was looking away. I followed his gaze and gaped in astonishment.

Marissa stood there, eye blazing and oozing magic. She had the short raven haired woman by the ass and throat held over her head on straight arms as if she weighed nothing. The woman struggled and power coursed out of her as actinic bolts of lightning that played harmlessly against Marissa. My eyes widened in surprise. My mistress was not shielding herself. Instead counter spells and dispels surged out of her in a rapid stream to block or redirect each and every attack. Her power cores kept her safe in a dazzling display of spell-work.

She locked eyes with Zandor and smirked before she knelt and brought the struggling woman down on her knee. Her back broke with an audible snap and she rolled to the ground with an anguished howl, her legs tumbling as she lost control.

Zandor roared and charged but Marissa calmly drew her revolver and shot the woman in the head. A heartbeat later the shot detonated and turned her head into skull fragments and brain.

Zandor roared again and kept charging. He stopped abruptly when a powerful shield appeared over us, trapping me, Marissa, Jani, Zandor and his three remaining apprentices within. An artificial ley line shot down from the focusing tower to power it with more power than we could possibly break through.

“Now we are trapped here, Zandor. Not even I can break this shield. This is the end for one of us,” she stated grimly.

“I’ll be fucked if I die before you,” Zandor snarled.

They raised their hands, magic coalescing to wreathe them in death.

###

They both threw their spells, thick rays of power that collided and exploded with bright embers that scorched the paving stones as they fell.

Marissa grimaced and leaned into her attack, but she was slowly being pushed back.

Zandor’s women howled and charged her, anything to aid their master.

“Jani! The bitches!” I screamed but I did not have time to check if she acted on my command for the blond woman was almost within reach.

I threw a fireball at her face and performed a one-handed chop with my axe at her leg.

As I had expected she flinched from the fireball and raised a shield to absorb it.

Out of the corner of my eye I saw Marissa push back Zandor’s spell a fraction. More spells shot out of them to arc wide to strike at the other but those attacks seemed more like distractions to make the others main assault falter so that their own could power through. My axe struck the thigh of the blond but her enchanted armour deflected it even though it appeared as if her flesh was bare to the night air.

I cursed myself for having forgotten that their enchantments would make it difficult indeed to harm them.

But that did not really matter. I was not truly aiming to kill her. All I had to do was to force her to use her magic to defend herself.

Any amount of power that she did not feed to Zandor would aid Marissa, and I knew that my mistress had in total less reserves than he did as long as he was connected to this bitch in front of me.

I roared and hurled my axe at the blond woman and she simply swayed to the side to dodge it.

But I had not been aiming at her. The redhead was behind her and my axe carried on toward the back of her head.

I knew that she would sense it coming and she shifted her shield to reinforce her back and my axe bounced harmlessly to the ground. But she stumbled from the feedback and in an instant Jani was there, her small hands wreathed in corrosive magic. Jani pushed her fists against the redheads shield and after a moments of resistance she broke through it in a blinding shower of embers. Her fingers latched onto the sides of her head and Jani’s face lit up as she poured malicious power into her victim.

The redhead screamed and flames belched out of her mouth and eyes. She went silent and limp and Jani shoved her away disdainfully just as the brunette bitch launched a bright bolt of force into her back. Jani’s back arched as her whole body stiffened and her scream cut through my ears.

I had no time to aid her for the blond was on me, spells flashing toward my chest. I deployed my shield and felt the spells strike it, the crystal glowing hot from having absorbed the spells.

Marissa’s arc of power had pushed back against Zandor now that his bitches were busy defending their own lives. But I could tell from the expression on her face that she was floundering. She would not last much longer.

I recalled my axe and imbued it with spell-breaking power before I launched myself at the blond woman. She was devilishly quick and my swing narrowly failed to sever the top of her head. It did sever the spikes on her enchanted crown and I grinned maliciously when I proved that their armour was not impenetrable. My axe was as enchanted, if not more, than their armour. And my enchantments had been made by a master in the art of enchantment. 

The best in the world.

I twirled my axe and attacked again, this time the woman twisting her body and leaning back, my axe nearly cutting of her tits. Before I could blink she had flipped around me, grabbing my upper arm and locking my axe away from harming her. Her blue eyes burned brightly and she glared twin streaks of green power at me. I just barely had enough time to twist my head to the side and searing pain stung my cheek.

I shifted my hips and brought my knee up with all of my might right into her cunt. Armour or no, that would hurt like a motherfucker.

She gasped and her jaw clenched as she stumbled back, her left hand groping between her legs.

My arm now freed from her grasp I did not bother swinging the axe head at her. I just reversed the direction and thrust with the pommel blade.

Her eyes widened in fear and she started to lean away but her savaged groin must have limited her mobility. I saw the cloud of pain in her eyes just before her movement stopped and my pommel blade slammed home right between her massive tits. Her armour absorbed the blow but the sheer force of my Blood Magic enhanced strike sent her reeling, stumbling away from me with her arms windmilling.

Power oozed out of her to arrest her fall and stabilized her, and I saw my opening. My axe was held in the perfect position already.

I slid my left hand up the haft until I nearly cradled the head and I reversed my grip, pivoting the axe along its central axis to deliver a powerful uppercut with the blade jutting from above my fist.

She saw it coming, of course, and she pushed with her feet to throw herself back.

But she had already used magic to stop her from falling backward and she pushed herself into her own unyielding magic cushion.

Her eyes widened, and then my enchanted axe blade punched through her chin and carried on.

Her eyes still stared at me, a wide furrow carved through her head from chin to forehead. Bone peeked through the gash and blood welled forth.

Those pretty blue eyes rolled back in her head and as her magic winked out of existence she fell to the paving stones with nary a sound. Power started surging out of her in the direction of Zandor and I recalled what Marissa had told me. Once they died the power that remained within them traveled through the umbilical to further empower Zandor.

I leaped to my knees beside the corpse and stuck my fingers through the crack of her skull. I ignored the wet softness of her brain against my fingertips and I pulled on her Soul, fighting the rough grip that Zandor had on it.

But I was in contact with the corpse and that gave me all the leverage that I would need. I ripped her Soul from her body, her form twisting and cracking before it finally burned and I devoured her Soul to power my own.

I stood up, invigorated and felt as if I was ready to take on the world on my own.

Jani was still fighting the brunette, but it was clear that my redheaded almost-lover had the upper hand. Jani was practically toying with her, only the enchanted armour of her opponent keeping the busty woman alive.

The woman was barely fighting back, focused entirely on defense. Jani could handle herself.

I spun to face Zandor and his eye twitched in my direction. Somehow he had managed to push Marissa back and I frowned. He should not have been able to do that. 

With a roar I threw my axe at him and with a disdainful twitch of his finger he raised a shield to block physical attacks.

But my axe had just been a distraction. As soon as I had let go of the handle and seen Zandor prepare his defense I spread my arms with a loud cry and expelled a large amount of the power that I had stolen from his whore. A multitude of bolts of power blasted out of my back and sides to arc toward Zandor to strike him from all directions.

I held no illusions that all of them would strike him, but he would have to spread his defense thin. And anything that detracted his power and focus from Marissa would be a boon to us. 

I recalled my axe and charged the towering man, tossing another spear of fire at his face.

He flinched hard when the flames reached for his face and Marissa’s attack pushed his back almost half a yard.

That was it! Big man feared fire.

I would to if I had been burned twice.

Even though he had been surprised by the sudden gout of fire he still managed to absorb or deflect most of my earlier attacks, yet the distraction cost him another few inches of attack.

Behind me Jani finished off her opponent by circumventing the defenses of her opponents armour entirely. With a clever spell she cast an airtight spell that covered her entire body, just outside of reach of her amour's dispelling field, and then she quickly pumped the air out of it. The brunette gasped silently for breath, her eyes bugging out and slowly turning red. She flailed and screamed soundlessly but nothing she could do physically would help. She lashed out with magic but her efforts were panicked and unfocused and Jani easily cast them down. She fluttered around her victim, one moment she was over there and the next moment she was somewhere else with a streak of magic. Her face was grim, her nose crinkled and eyes narrowed and with her teeth exposed in a disgusted but determined expression.

She was terrifying, and beyond beautiful.

Her weakening foe fell to her knees, her face turning from red to blue and then to all fours before she collapsed face first onto the paving stones. Once she was down Jani put her out of her misery quickly, ripping her enchanted diadem from her head before flattening it across the paving stones with a blast of concussive force.

While that had been going on, I had been sprinting headlong at Zandor, axe recalled to my hand and raised high, imbued with elemental fire. The axe head burned brightly, drawing Zandor’s eye.

Jani ghosted in on his other side and blasted his shield with raw magic showering both me and Zandor in the sparks and embers belched forth when his shield attempted to weather her assault. I swung my axe and to my surprise it only bounced slightly against his shield before it cut through. I turned my charge into a slide on one knee and twisted around, slicing the edge of my blade at the back of his leg. It did not bite deep, but deep enough to cause him to curse and stumble. Marissa’s spell was almost by his chest, yet he started to slowly push back when desperation started to set in.

Jani rose into the air to attack directly from above and I raised my axe again, but it was a feint. Just as his shield thickened behind his shoulder blades I instead dropped the axe and flung my hands out, twin jets of flame soaring through his shield that had been designed to deflect my axe. It cascaded across his armour and in the gaps between armour plates his shirt caught fire.

Jani dropped a massive spell from overhead that completely enveloped Zandor and Marissa’s arc of power suddenly speared into the mess of magic that surrounded the large man.

An anguished scream erupted from within the storm, and then it exploded. I was thrown through the air and I slammed hard into the barrier that Marissa had trapped us all inside. I groaned and pushed up from the ground, a series of pops traversing my spine painfully. Jani had been thrown straight up and was starting to turn back down but she caught herself shakily on her magic.

The dome was filled with magical static and my magesense was utterly confounded. I could see Marissa standing where she had been standing all through the fight, encased in a powerful personal shield. Her eyes were narrowed and she stared suspiciously into the area where Zandor had stood. It was still an indistinct fog of magical residue and Marissa’s crystal eye flashed rapidly as she cycled through her modes of vision.

Then her mouth fell open and her eyes widened when a shadow lumbered out of the cloud.

It was not possible! Nothing could have survived that discharge, yet there he was. Zandor lumbered out of the residual fog with a roar and somehow punched straight through her shield to clock Marissa on the jaw, sending her spinning away. I could hear the crack of her jaw shattering from where I was and I extended my hand and recalled my axe.

I exchanged a hopeless look with Jani before we charged the big man as one. 

He spun to face us and determined that Jani was the bigger threat and he launched a magical attack her.

He stumbled just as the spell flashed away from his hand, and that was when I saw it.

His armour was torn and ragged, the enchantments burnt out and dull. Blood oozed from a multitude of wounds and he seemed to be struggling to cast his spells.

He was still dangerous, but he was at the end of his rope. Without his women his power was waning fast. I slammed my axe into his belly but the crystal of his armour was enough to absorb the blow. He stumbled back from the sheer force of it but his backhand caught me in the neck and threw me to the side. Jani blasted in and lanced a thin ray of force through his shoulder before she was plucked bodily from the air and hurled to the side with a grunt.

Marissa leaped onto his back with a crystal dagger in hand that she raised high to punch into his neck. He reached up and caught her wrist before he twisted his torso and hurled her aside.

All of us was weakening and none of us had much magical strength left. The fight descended into a melee, only the odd spell was cast as a distraction or as a defensive maneuver. Even Jani had weakened enough to not zip through the air and she contented herself with wreathing her fists and forearms in magic to enhance her quick and rapid punches.

Zandor appeared to be struggling without any weapons. The fool had trusted his magic too much and his hubris would be his downfall.

My axe bit into the back of his thigh and he went down to one knee with a roar, yet he still managed to grab my gorget and pound his massive fist into the side of my head. 

It felt as if I had been struck by a train, again and again. My neck cracked ominously and it felt as if the muscles in my neck would tear at any moment. I flailed desperately, my hands searching for an eye to gouge but I just could not reach his face. The bastard was just too big.

Jani zipped in next to me, grabbing Zandor’s wrist and arm.

“Let him go you fucking asshole!” She screamed as her arms and hands lit up with enhancing magic.

With a savage jerk she broke Zandor’s elbow and twisted the forearm clear off. She tossed it aside and sent her flaming left jab into Zandor’s face. He head butted her hand and I watched in horror as her entire hand deformed when the mighty impact shattered the bones.

Jani screamed and fell back, clutching her ruined left hand with her right.

Red fury rose across my vision and I screamed, raised my axe and chopped it down on Zandor’s already wounded leg as he tried to rise. I cleaved his foot clean off and he fell back down.

Zandor heaved and punched me with all of his might, and a fair bit of magic, in my chest. I felt something crack and snap deep within me and then I was sliding across the paving stones, gasping for breath.

My lungs would not work, when I tried to breathe a searing pain knifed through me. I strained and looked down and saw that my armour and chest was caved in, a Zandor-fist sized indent in my chest. My head fell back and I could do nothing but lay there and watch the events unfold as I slowly died. Zandor glared angrily at his severed foot but then he turned his attention to Marissa. She was pushing up on all fours, shaking her head to clear it. Zandor crawled toward her with insane hatred shining out of his one good eye. He was going to pound her into a bloody paste, and then he was going to bleed out. Perhaps I would die before that. I turned my eyes toward Jani, but she was as defeated as I was. She sat with her back against the barrier, her destroyed hand cradled to her chest as blood dripped down her armour. She was magically spent and physically defeated. At least her wounds were not fatal. She would survive, but I doubted that her hand could be saved. But at least she would live.

I smiled at her, but her eyes were locked on Zandor as she watched in horror as he was on his way to kill his cousin.

I had to watch. I owed her that. I had failed my mistress, so I would have to witness her death before I gave in to my own.

My eyes traveled from Jani to Zandor and that was when I saw it. 

My axe.

My beautiful axe, meticulously and lovingly crafted by Marissa to be gifted to this monster. Or rather, to the sweet and loving man that I was certain that he had once been long ago.

It lay where I had dropped it, behind Zandor and directly opposite to me.

I strained with all of my might to move, but it was as if my body no longer truly responded to me.

Move, you fucking useless asshole! Don’t fail Marissa now, you piece of shit!

Move!

And miraculously, I did. 

I did not move much, I only raised my hand, but I did not need much more. 

With the last ounce of power that was left in my body I recalled my axe.

It twitched, and then it shot toward me, pommel first.

It took the shortest route from where it had been to my hand.

Right into Zandor. 

The pommel blade struck his weakened armour, pierced it and then sank deep into his ribs, the handle sinking halfway into his massive body.

Zandor gasped and gave a wheezing breath before he exhaled a mist of blood.

I must have fucked up his lung.

And still the huge asshole refused to go down. 

But it had slowed him down and Marissa finally pushed to her feet.

I saw that her crystal eye had finally shattered from the blow that had cracked her jaw and fragments of it had fallen out. Her mouth hang toward her chest in a shapeless lump, drool and blood dripping off her chin and I saw that she was missing almost an entire row of teeth.

She and Zandor stared into each other’s eye, one a pale green and full of vicious hatred in a snarling face.

The other soft and brown and regretful.

They did not exchange words. No final sentiment for one once loved and now turned bitter enemy.

Marissa drew her revolver in a single smooth movement and shot him through the head.

## CHAPTER SEVENTEEN Marissa

I watched as the ravaged husk of the man that I had once loved slowly toppled over with the finality of death.

He had been a terrifying juggernaut of destruction in life, yet when he lay on his side with his pale green eye staring wide at me I could somehow see past the horrific burns and I saw traces of the youth that I had so often cradled in my arms.

Yet, I felt nothing. Nothing at all for having killed him.

He was just another body in the near endless burial ground of corpses that I had personally put there.

Another plot of upturned soil among the thousands.

I had not wanted this, yet I was the cause of it. And now I had ended it.

I turned from his body and saw Jani curled into a ball at the edge of the shield, cradling her ruined hand to her chest.

She would live. I turned my gaze to my apprentice.

He was dead, a large dent in his breastplate and chest that had cratered his lungs and ribs.

A twinge of sorrow through my chest when I saw his broken body.

He had been young and full of potential, and now he had died because I had dragged him into a battle that was not his own.

A battle that he had once Sworn to never partake in.

The only reason that he was here was that I had superseded that Oath with my own.

A bubble of blood grew out of his nostril and then burst.

I was by his side in an instant, placing two fingers on his neck. There was a pulse there, weak and irregular.

“He lives,” I attempted to shout over my shoulder at Jani but my mangled jaw flopped uselessly and only a wet mewl escaped my mouth. Jani seemed to understand and she hurried to scramble over.

“The shield…” She whined with a look at he overhead nexus. “Open it,” she commanded me with more authority than I had believed would fit inside her petite body.

I shook my head with a rueful shrug. What I had told Zandor earlier had been the truth. I could not open the shield from the inside.

“Then you’ve killed him,” Jani sobbed and wrapped her long fingers of her intact hand around Barengar’s chin with loving tenderness.

I closed my eyes and felt my eyelid scrape painfully over the jagged edges of what remained of my crystal eye. I focused inward and saw that the crystal that had replaced my jawbone was shattered and beyond repair. I’d have to regrow it from scratch. But I would need to be able to speak before I had the chance to, so I decided to go for a quick fix. A telekinetic plane ripped through the tissue connecting to the crystal, slicing through to emulate my jaw. The hinge of my jaw was still intact and I attached the plane at either end before I laboriously separated the muscle from the few scraps of crystal that it had been attached to. I moved the tendon to my plane and formed a small clamp to fuse it to the temporary jaw I had created.

I opened my eye, and found that one of them had been locked, impaled on the jagged surface of my crystal eye.

I let out a sigh and worked my jaw. It was painful, but at least it worked well enough. I would not be able to eat with it, even if the teeth on that side had still been intact, but that would have to be fine.

The shield vanished around us, replaced by a slender woman of average height. Her face was lined and her hair was more gray than it’s original colour.

She was dressed in Bizhjerai battle garb and a sword and a revolver was slung on either hip.  
“But she can,” I grinned.

“Marie!” I called out to the Chief of the unified Clans. They’re wounded!”

Marie gave me a hard stare before she nodded and gestured for a squad of Bizhjerai to gather Jani and Barengar to get them to the healers.

Hopefully Barengar would live, but I had no time to go with him to make sure that he was fine.

I had Ca’el to kill.

Zandor may have been defeated, but his army of elite warriors still outnumbered the defenders by a fair margin, especially after Zandor and his whores had killed so many of them in their initial advance.

I rose to my full diminutive height and surveyed the area with my magesense.

Defending Bizhjerai had forced the invaders into another area of the outer city and set up improvised barriers where they took pot shots at the enemy. The breach appeared to have been reclaimed and only a limited number of the invaders had managed to get through.

But I knew that we were fucked. We did not have enough soldiers to man the walls and as soon as the invaders figured that out then they would abandon the breach and go over the top to swarm us on the inside.

“Let’s show these assholes what the Bizhjerai are made of!” 

The powerful voice echoed from the crystal buildings and I turned around in surprise. I had been so focused on the area before me that I had not sensed the large crowd approach behind me.

Civilians. Not a scrap of armour between the lot of them and most were armed with improvised cudgels and side-arms.

But there were a lot of them. It looked like most of the adult population of Dun Traelak had scrounged to find whatever weapons they could find to join in on the defense.

The man leading them was on the shorter side, and definitely on the chubbier side of being healthy. His prodigious gut hang down below his belt yet he shouldered an old single-shot musket as if it was a rod of command. An unlikely leader for a militia, but I would take it.

He jogged up to me, the throng of people milling behind him.

I saw a lot of scared faces, but all wore expressions of grim determination to defend their home despite the panic roiling in their bellies.

The fat man bowed low before me.

“Lady Marissa, we have come to defend our homes. Where do you need us?”’

I looked into his brown eyes and saw that he would not be dissuaded.

These people were not trained soldiers. Zandor’s elite would slaughter them en masse in a straight up firefight.

But giving up now… Losing Dun Traelak would cripple the Bizhjerai war machine.

Most likely many of these people would perish in the sacking of the city anyway.

I swept my magesense out behind me, surveying the area.

The Bizhjerai warriors were holding the breach and had the invading force contained. But Ca’el warriors were already scaling the walls.

I let out a sigh. “The walls. We must hold them at every price.”

The man nodded and gritted his teeth. He was old enough to remember the Skylander siege and he could very well have had parents who fought and perhaps died then.

He must have known what it would mean for him and his people if they climbed those walls.

My heart swelled with pride when he did not back down and he turned to address his people.

“Did you hear that, folks? We go over the top and get a chance to send those fucking Ca’el to the afterlife. Come with me, brothers and sisters, come with me and let us together put them to fucking grass!”

A roar of approval met his words and I had to reassess the fat man. He would have made an excellent Sergeant. Still might if he shed those extra pounds and trained properly.

“What is your name?” I asked him as I fell in beside him at a light jog.

“Maraus, my lady.”

“It is an honor to fight by your side, Maraus. You are a true exemplar of the Bizhjerai.”

His face flushed with embarrassment and honour. “I am just doing my duty, my lady. The enemy is at our door and I’ll be fucked if I let them lay hands on my family.”

“I can’t imagine what one would do to protect one’s children,” I mused as we ran toward the huge stairs that would lead us onto the walls.

“I have no idea. I ain’t got any. But she does,” he said and pointed to a slender woman that was on his heels. “He has three,” he pointed at a tall man further back. “And I believe that those two over there have six. They are all my children as well, my lady. We are all Bizhjerai.”

I stared straight ahead as his words rolled through me, down to my very core.

Ahead of me I saw the dark outline of a head poking above the lip of the wall and I fired my Angel jets and soared on top of the walls ahead of the throng and landed with a resounding clang of crystal heels on crystal blocks. The Ca’el warrior stared at me in surprise for half a heartbeat before I thrust my sword through his open mouth.

I reached down my other hand and grabbed a fistful of long and greasy hair before I triggered one of the enchantments on my sword. His head was sliced open along his jaw and his body tumbled down to smack brokenly into the ground far below. I stood up and turned around to display my grisly trophy to the approaching civilians.

“We are all Bizhjerai!” I screamed and I felt the words resonate within me, between the buildings.

“Bizhjerai!” The crowd responded as one and their pace increased.

More Ca’el emerged over the lip of the wall and I killed two of them. But they were too many and I soon found myself surrounded.

I knocked one off the wall with a sweep of my crystal wings and I listened to her scream end abruptly as she struck the ground outside.

A blade bounced off of my pauldron and I responded with a slash that opened the mans throat.

A bayonet found the gap between my gorget and pauldron and sank into the back of my shoulder. I twisted and backhanded the short woman across the face before I blasted her with a punch of magic that forced her mangled heart out from her back.

A powerful blow at the back of my head sent me reeling, only my helmet kept my skull intact. I could sense the man behind me, his rifle held high after he had struck me with the butt of the weapon and he smoothly brought it down to shoot me in the back.

The man flinched, a spark on his helmet twisting his head to the side and his shot went wide.

In front of me Maraus squeezed the trigger of his revolver again as he squinted down the crystal sights with one eye closed.

This time the bullet slammed into the top of my helmet and the force of the impact made the entire world shake and ring. I fell to my knee.

I felt hands on my shoulders, holding me steady.

“I’m so sorry, my lady,” Maraus blabbered next to me. “I’m sorry I thought I would hit him…” He continued on.

I shook my head to clear it and shrugged off his hands.

“Takes more than that to kill me,” I managed to get out.

All around me Bizhjerai were dying, but the implacable advance of the Ca’el had been stopped. They were not dropping at the rate that I had hoped, but at least there were not more of them gaining a foothold on top of this segment of wall. How the rest of the walls fared, I had no idea. But I had an inclination. The wall were far too long to hold even with the aid of the civilians. I gritted my teeth and stood up and searched for my dropped sword. I found it a few paces from me where it had been kicked by careless feet and by a flex of my will it soared into my hand.

The comforting presence of the hilt that I had gripped so many times, and even more times by my mother before me, soothed the aches in my body.

As long as I wielded this sword, I would fight. And while I fought, there were hope. As long as there is life, there is hope.

Only death is truly hopeless.

The longer we could stay alive, the longer we had for my contingency plan to kick in.

And so I fought. Time seemed to slow and speed up irregularly. I killed with magic, with revolver and sword.

Men and women fell to either side of me and foes fell before me. Cut after bruise after shot slammed into me but my mind seemed inundated to the pain. My armour was shattered, my flesh rent.

Yet I fought on. Blood was burning on my skin, sealing my wounds, but they kept coming quicker and quicker as my armour degraded.

Suddenly I saw a large group of Ca’el moving across the streets beneath us. On the wrong side of the wall.

We had failed to hold the length of the wall, as I had feared and expected.

“Fall back,” I tried to shout but the swelling of my jaw around my hasty reconstruction made it difficult to form words.

I cast about with my magesense in search of Maraus. His powerful voice would cut through the din of battle with ease.

I eventually found him sitting with his back to the parapets, fingers holding his belly in a vain attempt to keep his guts on the correct side of his skin. His head slumped and his eyes stared blankly into his lap.

“Fuck!” I groaned. We would soon be flanked and I really doubted that the civilians would be able to maintain their discipline once we were attacked in the rear. 

If our fight turned into a route then very few would survive.

I had seen proper routs before. That’s when the real killing began.

My Angel armour was fucked. I would die here with my people. I would have even if I had been able to retreat. I would not leave them behind, never scorn my people. Never again.

I had turned my back on them many years ago, and this was the result. Never again.

I raised my sword and charged to the front line to claim Ca’el blood.

###

It did not take long before I heard the panicked screams from behind.

The Ca’el were taking us in the rear, where the wounded would have retreated to. They would not stand a chance.

The Ca’el in front of me pressed their attack and I found myself pushed back by a shoulder in my tits. I fell and was promptly stomped on, a heavy combat boot descending into my midriff with the force of a crashing Skyship. I cried out in pain, and then the pressure released. I curled up into a ball, protecting my soft bits and trusting my helmet to keep my crystal skull intact.

It had been decades since I had felt this helpless. Not since I had been put in a cage in the Groundlands and put on public trial. Not since I had been forced to murder Aunt Alissa.

Yet, this was somehow worse.

Back then, my life had not mattered to anyone. I had been young and had left home.

If I died, then that would have been it.

A slow smile curled my lips beneath my visor. My life still did not matter. 

The school would prosper under Alana’s expert guidance. The Bizhjerai would flourish for as long as Marie lived and after her Anjali would most likely take over as Chief of the Unified Clans.

Epitome was well on her way to assume her true role in this world, mother’s sacrifice had accelerated her maturation process by decades if not centuries.

Everything would be just fine after I died.

I felt my body relax, giving up the struggle to stay alive.

A boot clomped down on my shin, snapping the crystal bones, yet I hardly felt the pain.

Pain was transient once you accepted death. It had been a constant through my entire life and now that I stood at death’s door… The magnitude of the pain was irrelevant. It would all end soon.

I longed for it.

My magesense lit up as a fiery meteor fell from the sky to crash into the soil outside of Dun Traelak.

The impact was massive and a huge cloud of dirt was thrown up high into the air.

The fighting around me stopped suddenly, everyone turning to look at the sudden arrival from the heavens.

Someone grabbed my gorget and laboriously dragged me across the blood-slick paving stones, grunting all the while.

Each tug on my body sent fresh agony coursing from my wounds, but it did not matter.

I had given everything I had to save Dun Traelak from this disaster that I had caused.

I had nothing more to give.

I was done.

A blast echoed out of the smoke and a Ca’el soldier fell before me, her head evacuated by a neat gunshot straight between the eyes.  
Screams of shock and surprise rang out all around me and the soldiers ducked.

An artificial ley line speared down from the heavens to connect to the object that had fallen and out of the smoke marched orderly ranks of crystal soldiers of the deepest indigo.

My lips curled faintly.

Epitome had come.

###

I woke up, once again in severe pain.

I groaned in disappointment. I had hoped that my life would have been over once I passed out on the parapet.

Once I had seen that Epitome had arrived to reinforce the Bizhjerai I had known that my people were safe I could have happily died.

I was disappointed that I had not. Someone had stripped me of my tattered armour and clothes, cleaned my body and wounds and treated me as best they could.

Most of my wounds had been superficial, it was only the sheer number of them that had been life threatening along with a severe case of magical exhaustion.

My many wounds had been cleaned, stitched up and bandaged with clean linen. My tattered eyelid had been removed and someone had taken a file to the remains of my eye to avoid further damage and my broken leg had been splinted. Apart from the pain I was in remarkably good health. A very malnourished health. While I slept my body had used Blood Magic to heal and since I had had no body fat left, not even in my breasts, it had started to consume my muscles in order to heal. I had atrophied quite a bit and my arms and legs were akin to sticks wrapped in cured leather. I was almost as run down as mother had been after her battle with the liche Grasax, though I would gauge my state to be slightly better than hers since my Soul was not torn to shreds as hers had been. I opened my eye and caught sudden movement out the corner of my vision. Cold crystal gripped my bony hand gently and raised it from the bed.

“Marissa! You’re alive!”

“I am, mother. Thanks for the assist,” I smiled gratefully.

My vision cleared and I was lost in the sight of my mother as she smiled down at me. Shining tears wept from her eyes yet her full lips were stretched in a gentle smile.

I squinted at her. Something was off about her. The set of her eyes were not quite as I remembered them and her smile was not as fierce. Her cheeks were a little rounder than they should have been.

I closed my eyes ruefully when I realized what was wrong.

It was not mother.

It was Epitome.

She looked so much like mother, and she acted so much like her.

Which was not strange since in an odd way she was my mother.

The core of my mother lived on inside Epitome, and would do so as long as Epitome was operational.

In a way, Epitome had become an afterlife akin to what the Bizhjerai believed. A place where your spirit lived on after death.

That thought warmed my heart. Mother was not gone, not completely. She had just changed.

My only regret about it was that mom and Kendall was not in there with her.

My eye sprung open at a sudden epiphany.

What if I could create an afterlife? A spell that bound to everyone and pulled their spirits into an artificial realm of bliss?

My mind spun, spells, runes and glyphs shining in my minds eye.

It would be similar to the Reliquary of Souls, but instead of dissecting the spirit it would simply house it in a crystal and interface it with an emulated world.

No one would truly have to die again.

I filed that project away for later. It was something worth perusing when I managed to get back to my workshop.

Epitome still smiled down at me.

“You will be pleased to learn that the hypothetical orbital deployment of avatars worked as expected. This trial gave me enough practical measurements to adjust the scanning procedure. It only took so long because of the advanced atmospheric calculations that had to be performed to ensure that the deployment was accurate. A deviation of the course could have been catastrophic for Dun Traelak,” Epitome reported even as the illusory tears of relief kept dripping down her cheeks.

“Good to hear,” I said and I tried to sit up. It was a monumental task and sweat broke out across my body almost immediately. My atrophied muscles burned with effort yet I barely managed to raise my limbs an inch from the bed.

I let my head fall into the pillow with a heavy thunk and I lay there panting for breath.

Crippled.

In time I might be able to regrow my body with extensive use of Blood Magic, copious amounts of sustenance and a grueling training regime. But if I could not even move my limbs that would be very difficult to even get started with.

Tears of frustration welled out of my eye and pooled across the broken surface of my crystal orb.

I had been ready to die, willing even, out on the walls of Dun Traelak. But the idea of a magically powered afterlife had rekindled the withering flame inside my chest.

I might still serve some purpose to the world before I let out my final breath.

“Don’t cry, Marissa,” Epitome murmured and stroked my hair in a gesture that was so like mother that I almost broke down completely.

Oh, how I wished that I could once again collapse into her strong arms and find shelter pressed tightly against her chest. To inhale the scent of sweat, magic crystal and blood that had always surrounded her.
