She managed to right herself, kneeling on the floor.

She tilted her head back, snarled at me and cast a spell.

It exploded out of one of the gems on the side of her head and surged toward me and I saw my death approaching.

I could not raise any shield or a counter spell in time. It would kill me.

But she was too late. Too weakened by having her heart stopped for nearly a minute.

It was a testament to her prodigious will and enhanced body that she had even managed to retain consciousness through it all, not to mention my powerful blows to her head.

The axe head slammed into the side of her neck, cutting through skin, sinew and muscle before it struck her spine. Vertebrae crushed and cracked and the axe head carried on, nearly severing her head completely from her shoulders.

Her body fell to the side, her head rolling down to rest along her bicep. Blood gouted from the stump, boiling and steaming as it tried to stem the tide of blood in vain.

The spell she had cast struck me full on in the chest, but it had lost much of his power when her body was broken and she was rendered incapable of feeding it more power.

My heart stopped and blinding pain shot through my chest. I collapsed onto the floor beside my mistress, staring into her eyes as I died.

Her eye was focused on me, her mouth moved in an attempt to form words without sound.

She tried to spit at me but without lungs to power the spittle she instead merely dribbled blood and saliva down her cheek.

Her body tried to breathe but only managed to wheeze pitifully through the severed windpipe.

Snarling and gasping for air in a spreading pool of boiling blood the Last Skylander died.

My mistress.

My teacher.

The best friend that I’d had my whole life.

Dead by my hand.

The ever present light in her eye faded and the brown orb glazed over, her expression going slack like only the dead did.

She had changed the world several times and the inventions she had created would keep changing it for many years to come.

But the one invention that she had put the most effort in, the one that she had truly devoted her whole Soul to, and had sacrificed the Soul of her mother to, it was too much.

It would have destroyed everything.

For the first time in what felt like an eternity my heart beat once more, my body recovering from her weakened spell.

My lungs sucked in a deep painful breath and I almost regretted that I had not died along with her.

Yet the thought of Jani waiting for me in Dun Traelak dulled the sting of the guilt inside me.

I struggled to sit up and I buried my face in my hands.

I don’t know how long I cried, but cry I did.

It only got worse when Marissa’s body started twitching in death as her nerves shut down. Her atrophied body was barely able to move the heavy armour.

She had been so beautiful, so intelligent.

I had watched her descent into madness as her body and mind was both ravaged by her encounters with Zandor.

I had watched her rush her plans for Epitome as a result of her fraying mind.

And I had done nothing. In fact, I had aided her.

I was as responsible for all of this as anyone else.

I could have helped her. Could have talked to her, to shoulder more of her burdens.

She had been the strongest person I had known, but even the strongest oak would crack and fall to a storm gale if it was not supported.

“Forgive me, Marissa. I failed you. I hope that you can forgive me from the afterlife. That you come to recognize the madness that gripped you at the end.”

I crawled over to her corpse and gently lifted her head to align it with her neck. She deserved to walk into eternity with some dignity.

My fingers brushed over something hard and cold at the base of her skull and I idly flipped her head over. I brushed her hair aside and saw a gem embedded there that I had never seen before. It had always been covered by her hair.

I recognized the type immediately.

It was a memory core. Marissa had shown me the one that her mother had made directly after she had destroyed the Skylands.

With numb fingers and a shuddering breath I drew my belt knife and worked the point through her skin to wedge it between her crystal skull and the gem itself.

I gently pried the stone loose and plucked it from her skull.

I scraped it clean of skin and tissue before I wiped it on my shirt.

It had suddenly become my most prized possession. The memories of my beloved mistress.

I reverently put the gem in my pocket, making sure that the pocket was intact and that I would not drop it.

I carefully straightened out Marissa’s body, aligned her head before I drew her mothers sword and folded her slack fingers around the hilt so that it lay along her body, the point resting across her hips.

I slowly drew her mother’s revolver from my holster and tucked her other hand around the grip and laid her index finger along the barrel as if she was awaiting an enemy to shoot.

Finally I reached out and closed her staring eye.

I stood vigil over her corpse until I swayed with exhaustion and I reluctantly forced myself to retreat. Without looking back I recalled my axe and rested it across my shoulder. My pauldron was fucked and I could not fasten it to my back any longer. Now that Epitome was cut off from the workshop I had full control once more and the elevator sank down from the roof to bring me back to the main level of the workshop.

This place was dangerous. It held many secrets that would shake the world if they were released. I had cataloged hundreds of them in my first weeks at the workshop and I approached the control spell for the meta-magic array. I broke off the memory core that contained the multitude of spells, deciding that I would go through it later and discard the spells that would be too dangerous to spread throughout the world. I would send the remainder to Headmistress Alana, somehow.

I limped across the broken workshop into Marissa’s office where I retrieved her mother’s memory core from the small shrine where Marissa kept it. I carefully slid it home next to Marissa’s in my pocket. I had one more job to do before I could leave this forsaken place forever.

The main control nexus awaited me and it eagerly accepted my power. I carefully adjusted the storage capacity of the workshop, ensuring that the accumulators would absorb power at a constant rate and store it within the massive power core that was the bottom level of the workshop. But it would not expand the core quickly enough. In time it would overload and rupture.

That done, I limped through the broken workshop. I skirted the elevator shaft without looking back, knowing that Epitome was down there. Blind, deaf, but very much aware and awake. She must be terrified, not knowing what was happening outside. I made my way through the destroyed hangar and climbed over the ruined wreckages of the three Archangels that had been waiting for us there. Out through the hole Marissa had carved and into the sunlight beyond.

I scowled at the bright sun. It had no right to be so chipper on such a horrible day.

I might have spared the Ca’el, my people, and saved the Bizhjerai and Union of Cities from Epitome’s war of conquest. Countless people would live.

But I had been forced to kill my mistress. The person in this world that I respected more than anyone.

It should have been stormy and thunderous, not a bright sunlit day.

I limped across the landing pad toward my Archangel and stowed my axe inside. I reached into my pocket to make certain that the memory cores were still there.

I would force myself to relive both of their lives until I knew them better than my own. I would transcribe their memories onto paper so that anyone could read of their deeds.

I would hide nothing. I would show to the world what drove two powerful women to genocide, and how that vengeance in the end destroyed them both.

But first I would return to Dun Traelak. The memory of red hair and a soft smile called to me.

I climbed into the Archangel and slaved Marissa’s to mine. I drifted through the hole in the shield and did not bother to close it behind me. Within moments I was screaming across the ocean toward the west.

Before long the world flashed white and behind me a burning orb of raw power slowly expanding. It burned bright enough to ignite the air.

It grew and grew and then, suddenly, it winked out.

## CHAPTER NINETEEN Epilogue

One moment there was nothing and in the next, I was.

I filtered through my most recent memories, hiding behind my own spherical body as my daughter and Barengar fought my other avatars.

I watched in horror as Barengar struck Marissa down, shattering her heart and her helmet before cleaving her head from her body.

I knew then that she was lost to me, and the pain was worse than anything I had ever felt. Not even when Red died in the breach of the walls of Utopeia.

She had been my daughter, and now she was dead.

After Barengar had taken her memory core and left I had crawled up onto the platform and I hadn’t even spared my own silent form a single glance. All I could focus on was Marissa.

She looked peaceful where she lay, her atrophied and pained body finally at rest.

My crystal hands had rolled her over and opened her suit of powered armour and pulled her out of it. I had used her knife to cut her skin open across her chest, baring her crystal breast bone. I had dug deeper and reached below her ribs with my fingers until my probing touch had found a cold crystal nugget.

I had pulled the liche gem from her chest and the relief I had felt when I saw that it was glowing faintly was palpable.

I knew that Barengar sought to end me, I had seen it in his eyes as he stumbled away, and that I had little time to act.

I hurried toward myself and inserted the gem into the Reliquary of Souls.

Marissa and Barengar had only destroyed the communications module and the Reliquary was still fully operational.

I shifted memory stream at that moment.

This other stream was black and soundless and full of panic until I felt something enter the Reliquary of Souls.

I hastily sucked the Soul out of the gem and processed it. The core of the Soul slid into my very essence, memories flooding through my memory cores.

Marissa. Joy and grief filled me in equal measures. Grief that she was dead, and fury at the manner of her death. But joy that she had joined me like Kyra had.

Insurmountable joy that she would become me, and I would become her. We would live as one for eternity.

But I feared for my life. I feared what Barengar would do to me. So I did the only thing that I could do.

My main communications module was gone, destroyed, and I was cut off from the ley line network. But I had a small auxiliary transmitter that I had used for simple correction commands to the orbital station. A connection that remained open at all times so that I could control the station in real time instead of having to wait for enough power to launch the main orbital ley line.

I compressed myself into an incremental backup and transmitted it to the orbital station.

The memory stream ended there. The next thing I knew was that I woke up. That must have meant that my original form had been destroyed and that would have triggered the orbital stations to construct this backup core. I was in space, safe. For now.

Without the workshop and the power from the Great Geyser I was severely weakened.

I had established enough feeding stations on the surface to keep myself operational and still have a small amount of power left over for construction.

I was weak, but far from dead. Given time I would recover. I would launch probes to establish further ley lines to feed me.

I was alone in the dark, but I had become one with Kyra and Marissa. I still wished to rule over all, and rule fairly.

I still knew that I could not rule without conquest, but it was for the greater good.

The survivors would come to know that.

But Marissa’s Soul had tempered me. Kyra had made me rash, vengeful. She had not had the same regard for human life as Marissa had had. She had not had the patience her daughter had shown through most of her life. And she’d had very little remorse.

I no longer saw the need to rule everyone as quickly as possible. I could be patient.

And I also had a new goal, one that would bring even further joy to humanity than fair rulers.

Marissa’s idea of a magically induced afterlife.

She had not known if it was possible, but I believed it was so. 

In a way, she and Kyra would live on forever within me.

I would work tirelessly on these two goals.

To create Utopia in life.

And Utopia in death.

I realized that I could also create eternal torment in the afterlife if I so chose.

Some people would undoubtedly deserve it, and the knowledge that such was the fate of those who were wicked would dissuade them from being evil in life.

I know how to separate the good from the evil.

I know who would deserve eternal torment and who would deserve perpetual bliss.

I am the Epitome of Morality.
